### Dual-branch Prompting for Multimodal Machine Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17588v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Wang, Zhendong Yang, Liansong Zong, Xiaobo Zhang, Dexian Wang, Ji Zhang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Machine Translation (MMT) typically enhances text-only translation by incorporating aligned visual features. Despite the remarkable progress, state-of-the-art MMT approaches often rely on paired image-text inputs at inference and are sensitive to irrelevant visual noise, which limits their robustness and practical applicability. To address these issues, we propose D2P-MMT, a diffusion-based dual-branch prompting framework for robust vision-guided translation. Specifically, D2P-MMT requires only the source text and a reconstructed image generated by a pre-trained diffusion model, which naturally filters out distracting visual details while preserving semantic cues. During training, the model jointly learns from both authentic and reconstructed images using a dual-branch prompting strategy, encouraging rich cross-modal interactions. To bridge the modality gap and mitigate training-inference discrepancies, we introduce a distributional alignment loss that enforces consistency between the output distributions of the two branches. Extensive experiments on the Multi30K dataset demonstrate that D2P-MMT achieves superior translation performance compared to existing state-of-the-art approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces D2P-MMT, a novel dual-branch prompting framework for multimodal machine translation (MMT). D2P-MMT addresses the limitations of existing MMT approaches that rely on paired image-text inputs at inference and are sensitive to irrelevant visual noise. Instead, D2P-MMT uses only the source text and a reconstructed image generated by a pre-trained diffusion model.  During training, the model jointly learns from both authentic and reconstructed images using a dual-branch prompting strategy, encouraging rich cross-modal interactions. A distributional alignment loss is introduced to bridge the modality gap and mitigate training-inference discrepancies. Experiments on the Multi30K dataset demonstrate superior translation performance compared to existing state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has several novel aspects.

    *   The use of a diffusion model to reconstruct images from the source text for MMT is innovative. This addresses the sensitivity to visual noise and the reliance on paired images at inference, which are limitations of many prior works.
    *   The dual-branch prompting strategy, using both authentic and reconstructed images during training, is a clever way to encourage robust learning.  It allows the model to learn from potentially noisy real images while being guided by the cleaner, reconstructed images.
    *   The distributional alignment loss is a standard, but necessary, technique to ensure the reconstructed images behave similarly to authentic images.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a practical problem in MMT: the reliance on paired images at inference and sensitivity to noise. By generating images from text and using a dual-branch training approach, the method improves robustness and broadens the applicability of MMT systems. The reported experimental results demonstrate improved performance on a standard benchmark.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a critical limitation in MMT.
    *   The use of diffusion models for image reconstruction in this context is a good idea.
    *   The dual-branch prompting strategy is well-motivated and effective.
    *   Experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper relies on a pre-trained Stable Diffusion model. While this is a reasonable choice given the complexity of training such models, it limits the method's applicability in scenarios where access to such a model is restricted or where specific domain knowledge needs to be incorporated into the image generation process. However, it is explicitly stated in the paper, and considered as a part of the architecture as a whole.
    *   The Multi30K dataset is relatively small and simple.  While it is a standard benchmark, the results may not fully generalize to more complex datasets and real-world scenarios.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence future research in MMT by demonstrating the effectiveness of reconstruction-based approaches and dual-branch training strategies. It opens up avenues for exploring other generative models and techniques for improving the robustness and applicability of MMT systems.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7.5. The paper introduces a novel approach to MMT with several strong contributions: the use of reconstructed images via diffusion models, the dual-branch prompting strategy, and the distributional alignment loss. The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods on the Multi30K dataset. However, reliance on a pre-trained Stable Diffusion limits deployment flexibility and the relatively small scale of Multi30K leaves questions about generalizability on more diverse datasets. Nevertheless, the paper offers a promising direction for future research in MMT and addresses limitations of previous work, justifying a strong, but not exceptional, rating.
Score: 7.5

### Vision Transformer attention alignment with human visual perception in aesthetic object evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17616v1)
- **Authors**: Miguel Carrasco, César González-Martín, José Aranda, Luis Oliveros
- **Abstract**: Visual attention mechanisms play a crucial role in human perception and aesthetic evaluation. Recent advances in Vision Transformers (ViTs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in computer vision tasks, yet their alignment with human visual attention patterns remains underexplored, particularly in aesthetic contexts. This study investigates the correlation between human visual attention and ViT attention mechanisms when evaluating handcrafted objects. We conducted an eye-tracking experiment with 30 participants (9 female, 21 male, mean age 24.6 years) who viewed 20 artisanal objects comprising basketry bags and ginger jars. Using a Pupil Labs eye-tracker, we recorded gaze patterns and generated heat maps representing human visual attention. Simultaneously, we analyzed the same objects using a pre-trained ViT model with DINO (Self-DIstillation with NO Labels), extracting attention maps from each of the 12 attention heads. We compared human and ViT attention distributions using Kullback-Leibler divergence across varying Gaussian parameters (sigma=0.1 to 3.0). Statistical analysis revealed optimal correlation at sigma=2.4 +-0.03, with attention head #12 showing the strongest alignment with human visual patterns. Significant differences were found between attention heads, with heads #7 and #9 demonstrating the greatest divergence from human attention (p< 0.05, Tukey HSD test). Results indicate that while ViTs exhibit more global attention patterns compared to human focal attention, certain attention heads can approximate human visual behavior, particularly for specific object features like buckles in basketry items. These findings suggest potential applications of ViT attention mechanisms in product design and aesthetic evaluation, while highlighting fundamental differences in attention strategies between human perception and current AI models.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper and can provide a summary and critical evaluation.

**Summary**

This paper investigates the correlation between human visual attention, measured via eye-tracking, and the attention mechanisms of Vision Transformers (ViTs) when evaluating aesthetic objects.  Specifically, the study focuses on handcrafted objects (basketry bags and ginger jars) and analyzes the alignment of human gaze patterns with the attention maps generated by a pre-trained ViT model using DINO (Self-DIstillation with NO Labels).  The researchers conducted an eye-tracking experiment with 30 participants and compared human and ViT attention distributions using Kullback-Leibler divergence across varying Gaussian parameters.  The results indicate that while ViTs exhibit a more global attention pattern, certain attention heads can approximate human visual behavior, particularly for specific object features. The study identifies specific attention heads within the ViT that show greater alignment with human visual patterns and finds that the optimal correlation occurs at a particular Gaussian parameter. The paper concludes by discussing the potential applications of ViT attention mechanisms in product design and aesthetic evaluation while acknowledging the fundamental differences in attention strategies between human perception and current AI models.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in directly comparing human eye-tracking data during aesthetic object evaluation with the attention maps generated by Vision Transformers. While there have been previous studies comparing ViTs with human vision, focusing on aesthetic object evaluation adds a specific and relevant context. The use of handcrafted objects, with their inherent complexities and aesthetic considerations, provides a more nuanced testing ground than generic image datasets.  The exploration of different attention heads within the ViT and the optimization of Gaussian parameters for comparison are also valuable contributions. However, the approach is somewhat incremental; it builds upon existing research comparing CNNs to human vision, simply using a more current architecture.

**Significance:** The significance of this research lies in its potential to bridge the gap between AI and human perception in aesthetic contexts. Identifying ViT components that correlate with human attention could lead to more effective AI-driven design tools and aesthetic evaluation systems.  Understanding the limitations of ViTs in mimicking human attention, as highlighted in the paper, is also crucial for guiding future AI development. The findings suggest potential applications in areas like product design, advertising, and art analysis. However, the study is limited by a relatively small sample size (30 participants) and a specific set of objects. The conclusions are somewhat preliminary and require further validation with larger and more diverse datasets. While the finding that certain attention heads correlate better with human vision is interesting, the underlying reasons for this behavior are not thoroughly explored, which limits the actionable insights. The paper acknowledges a connection, but doesn't delve into a *causal* explanation or provide a clear direction for future architectural improvement.

**Strengths:**

*   Clear research question and methodology.
*   Direct comparison of human eye-tracking data and ViT attention maps.
*   Focus on a specific and relevant context (aesthetic object evaluation).
*   Detailed analysis of ViT attention heads and optimization of comparison parameters.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Relatively small sample size.
*   Limited diversity of objects used in the experiment.
*   Lack of in-depth explanation for the observed correlations.
*   The novelty is incremental rather than groundbreaking.
*   Doesn't fully address the underlying differences between human and AI attention models.
*   Limited investigation of *why* certain heads align better with human attention. This undermines the potential for designing better architectures.
*   Analysis can be more comprehensive, considering the limitations of human attention models that only consider feed-forward processes.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the points above, a score of **6** is appropriate. The paper offers a solid, incremental contribution to the field by comparing ViT attention to human visual attention in the specific domain of aesthetic evaluation. The methodology is sound, and the results are interesting and potentially useful. However, the limitations regarding sample size, lack of in-depth analysis of the "why", the incremental nature of the contribution, and unexplored nuances associated with limitations of AI and human attention models prevent it from being considered a more significant breakthrough. The paper is a good step forward but needs further research to realize its full potential.

### A Hybrid Early-Exit Algorithm for Large Language Models Based on Space Alignment Decoding (SPADE)
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17618v1)
- **Authors**: Bowen Zheng, Ming Ma, Zhongqiao Lin, Tianming Yang
- **Abstract**: Large language models are computationally expensive due to their deep structures. Prior research has shown that intermediate layers contain sufficient information to generate accurate answers, leading to the development of early-exit algorithms that reduce inference costs by terminating computation at earlier layers. However, these methods often suffer from poor performance due to misalignment between intermediate and output layer representations that lead to decoding inaccuracy. To address these challenges, we propose SPADE (SPace Alignment DEcoding), a novel decoding method that aligns intermediate layer representations with the output layer by propagating a minimally reduced sequence consisting of only the start token and the answer token. We further optimize the early-exit decision-making process by training a linear approximation of SPADE that computes entropy-based confidence metrics. Putting them together, we create a hybrid early-exit algorithm that monitors confidence levels and stops inference at intermediate layers while using SPADE to generate high-quality outputs. This approach significantly reduces inference costs without compromising accuracy, offering a scalable and efficient solution for deploying large language models in real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel hybrid early-exit algorithm for large language models (LLMs) called SPADE-EXIT (SPace Alignment DEcoding). The algorithm addresses the computational expense of LLMs by terminating inference at intermediate layers. It introduces SPADE, a decoding method that aligns intermediate layer representations with the output layer by propagating a minimally reduced sequence (start token and answer token) through the remaining layers.  To make early-exit decisions efficiently, it also proposes L-SPADE, a linear approximation of SPADE used for confidence estimation based on entropy. By monitoring confidence levels with L-SPADE and using SPADE for output generation, the algorithm reduces inference costs without sacrificing accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the SPADE decoding method.  It offers a different approach compared to existing methods like Logit Lens or Tuned Lens, which rely on linear transformations or auxiliary training signals.  Propagating a minimal sequence to align intermediate and output spaces seems promising. L-SPADE offers a computationally cheaper approximation, making early-exit decisions feasible. However, the core early-exit idea of confidence-based termination isn't entirely novel.

* **Significance:** The potential significance is in providing a practical and scalable method for deploying large language models efficiently.  The ability to reduce inference costs without significant accuracy loss is crucial for wider adoption. If the method generalizes well across tasks and models, it could have a meaningful impact. However, it remains to be seen how it scales and performs with significantly larger models.

* **Strengths:**
    * The SPADE decoding approach offers a novel way to bridge the gap between intermediate and output layer representations without extensive training.
    * L-SPADE makes the confidence estimation process efficient enough for practical use in early exiting.
    * The hybrid SPADE-EXIT algorithm effectively combines accuracy and speed by leveraging both SPADE and L-SPADE.
    * Experimental results on ARC, BoolQ, and HeadQA datasets show promising performance improvements and demonstrate the method's potential. The added Vicuna experiments strengthen the claims.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * While SPADE claims to avoid auxiliary training, L-SPADE *does* require training. The term "no auxiliary training" can be misleading.
    * The experiments primarily focus on relatively smaller datasets. Assessing the performance on more complex and larger datasets is necessary to validate the method's robustness.
    * The limitation that the algorithm is focused on single-token generation limits its direct applicability in many applications, and the extension is acknowledged as a challenge.
    * The benefits of using SPADE in conjunction with L-SPADE compared to more simpler method in extracting intermediate layer representations and early exit is not significant.

* **Impact:** If the method proves scalable and generalizable, it could influence future research on efficient LLM inference. It might inspire other approaches that focus on aligning representational spaces without relying solely on linear transformations.

* **Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**  The paper presents a novel approach to early exiting by introducing SPADE and L-SPADE. The empirical evaluation, while limited in scope, indicates performance gains in accuracy and inference speed. However, the reliance on the single token generation limits its impact on diverse tasks. Moreover, while SPADE seems to alleviate the need of extra training, L-SPADE does require it. A thorough assessment of robustness is necessary to assert the benefits.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Who Attacks, and Why? Using LLMs to Identify Negative Campaigning in 18M Tweets across 19 Countries
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17636v1)
- **Authors**: Victor Hartman, Petter Törnberg
- **Abstract**: Negative campaigning is a central feature of political competition, yet empirical research has been limited by the high cost and limited scalability of existing classification methods. This study makes two key contributions. First, it introduces zero-shot Large Language Models (LLMs) as a novel approach for cross-lingual classification of negative campaigning. Using benchmark datasets in ten languages, we demonstrate that LLMs achieve performance on par with native-speaking human coders and outperform conventional supervised machine learning approaches. Second, we leverage this novel method to conduct the largest cross-national study of negative campaigning to date, analyzing 18 million tweets posted by parliamentarians in 19 European countries between 2017 and 2022. The results reveal consistent cross-national patterns: governing parties are less likely to use negative messaging, while ideologically extreme and populist parties -- particularly those on the radical right -- engage in significantly higher levels of negativity. These findings advance our understanding of how party-level characteristics shape strategic communication in multiparty systems. More broadly, the study demonstrates the potential of LLMs to enable scalable, transparent, and replicable research in political communication across linguistic and cultural contexts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces the use of zero-shot Large Language Models (LLMs) for cross-lingual classification of negative campaigning in political communication. It argues that LLMs overcome limitations of traditional methods (expert surveys, manual coding, supervised machine learning) which are either expensive, difficult to scale cross-nationally, or require extensive labeled training data. The authors evaluate LLM performance against manually coded datasets in ten languages, demonstrating performance comparable to or exceeding human coders and supervised methods.  They then apply this method to analyze 18 million tweets from European parliamentarians across 19 countries, identifying patterns in negative campaigning based on party characteristics like governing experience, ideological extremism, and populism.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application and rigorous evaluation of LLMs for the specific task of negative campaign classification, especially in a cross-lingual context. While LLMs have been used for text classification before, this paper directly addresses the challenges of comparative political communication research, where data scarcity and linguistic diversity have been major obstacles. The explicit comparison against human coders and existing machine learning baselines is a strong point. The study represents a significant contribution in adopting a readily available technology in a relatively novel manner.

*   **Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from its potential to transform comparative political communication research. The ability to analyze large, multilingual datasets at a fraction of the cost and effort of traditional methods opens up new avenues for investigating political communication dynamics across diverse contexts. The findings regarding party characteristics and negative campaigning in Europe offer valuable insights into strategic communication in multiparty systems, moving beyond the US-centric focus of much existing research.  The paper provides empirical evidence demonstrating LLMs can deliver, a key step towards building confidence and adoption in this research area.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The paper provides a strong empirical evaluation of LLM performance against high-quality manually coded datasets.
    *   **Large-Scale Application:**  The analysis of 18 million tweets represents a substantial contribution to the empirical study of negative campaigning.
    *   **Clear Theoretical Framework:**  The study is grounded in a well-defined theoretical framework related to strategic incentives and party behavior.
    *   **Addresses a Significant Gap:** It directly addresses the limitations of existing methods in comparative political communication research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Generalizability Assumptions:**  The study assumes that LLM classification accuracy generalizes to the languages not directly validated. While the authors address this concern with a robustness check, the lack of direct validation for all languages is a limitation.
    *   **Temporal Imprecision:** The temporal mismatch between Twitter data and CHES data introduces a degree of imprecision. This is acknowledged but could be further explored.
    *   **Data Limitations**: The study only focuses on parties represented in national parliaments which ignores smaller or emerging parties which may exhibit greater negative campaigning.
    *   **Potential Bias**: While zero-shot prompting reduces the reliance on labeled data, the bias of LLMs themselves is important to consider. This is briefly mentioned but could be discussed in more detail.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research by:
    *   Demonstrating the feasibility and value of using LLMs for cross-lingual text classification in political communication.
    *   Encouraging more comparative research on negative campaigning and other political communication phenomena.
    *   Providing a template for evaluating and applying LLMs in other research domains.

The paper convincingly demonstrates the value of LLMs in overcoming longstanding challenges in political communications research. However, the acknowledged limitations regarding language generalizability and temporal imprecision prevents the paper from achieving a higher score. It may be interesting for future research to attempt fine-tuning these LLMs to further improve accuracy in individual languages.

### CNS-Bench: Benchmarking Image Classifier Robustness Under Continuous Nuisance Shifts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17651v1)
- **Authors**: Olaf Dünkel, Artur Jesslen, Jiahao Xie, Christian Theobalt, Christian Rupprecht, Adam Kortylewski
- **Abstract**: An important challenge when using computer vision models in the real world is to evaluate their performance in potential out-of-distribution (OOD) scenarios. While simple synthetic corruptions are commonly applied to test OOD robustness, they often fail to capture nuisance shifts that occur in the real world. Recently, diffusion models have been applied to generate realistic images for benchmarking, but they are restricted to binary nuisance shifts. In this work, we introduce CNS-Bench, a Continuous Nuisance Shift Benchmark to quantify OOD robustness of image classifiers for continuous and realistic generative nuisance shifts. CNS-Bench allows generating a wide range of individual nuisance shifts in continuous severities by applying LoRA adapters to diffusion models. To address failure cases, we propose a filtering mechanism that outperforms previous methods, thereby enabling reliable benchmarking with generative models. With the proposed benchmark, we perform a large-scale study to evaluate the robustness of more than 40 classifiers under various nuisance shifts. Through carefully designed comparisons and analyses, we find that model rankings can change for varying shifts and shift scales, which cannot be captured when applying common binary shifts. Additionally, we show that evaluating the model performance on a continuous scale allows the identification of model failure points, providing a more nuanced understanding of model robustness. Project page including code and data: https://genintel.github.io/CNS.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CNS-Bench, a novel benchmark for evaluating the robustness of image classifiers under continuous nuisance shifts.  Unlike existing benchmarks that use either simple synthetic corruptions or are limited to binary shifts, CNS-Bench uses diffusion models, enhanced with LoRA adapters, to generate realistic images with a wide range of individual nuisance shifts at varying severity levels.  The authors also propose an improved filtering mechanism to remove out-of-class samples during the benchmarking process.  Using CNS-Bench, they conduct a large-scale study evaluating the robustness of over 40 classifiers across different architectures, model sizes, and pre-training paradigms.  The analysis reveals that model rankings can change depending on the specific shift and its magnitude, highlighting the importance of considering continuous shifts. The paper also shows that evaluating models on a continuous scale allows for the identification of failure points, providing a more nuanced understanding of robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *continuous* nature of the nuisance shifts. Existing benchmarks mostly deal with discrete or binary shifts.  While the idea of using generative models for robustness evaluation isn't entirely new, the use of LoRA adapters for *continuous control* of these shifts for a large number of ImageNet classes is a significant advancement.  The proposed filtering method also contributes to the practicality of the approach. It enables automated cleaning of generated datasets.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the more realistic evaluation it enables.  Real-world scenarios often involve gradual changes rather than sudden shifts. This makes CNS-Bench a more relevant tool for assessing model performance in practical applications.  The large-scale study provides valuable insights into the relative robustness of different model families and training techniques, potentially influencing future research directions.  The finding that model rankings are *shift-dependent* is a key takeaway, demonstrating the limitations of relying on single, aggregate robustness metrics. Identifying failure points and evaluating the severity of accuracy degradation are highly desirable.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Realistic and controllable shifts:** Diffusion models with LoRA adapters allow for realistic and fine-grained control over nuisance shifts.
    *   **Scalability:** The method is scalable in terms of the number of classes and types of shifts considered.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The large-scale study covers a wide range of models and reveals important trends.
    *   **Improved filtering mechanism:** Increases the reliability of the benchmarking process.
    *   **Clear presentation and insightful analysis:** The paper is well-written, and the analysis is thorough and insightful.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on generative models:** The benchmark's fidelity is inherently limited by the generative model used (Stable Diffusion).  While LoRA adapters help, there's a potential for biases or artifacts from the generation process to affect the results. It is possible that the generated data can create biases by deviating from ImageNet.
    *   **Computational cost:** Training LoRA adapters for each class and shift is computationally expensive, although the evaluation itself is likely more efficient than collecting and annotating real-world OOD data.

*   **Potential Influence:** CNS-Bench has the potential to become a valuable resource for the computer vision community, influencing the design of more robust image classifiers.  The insights gained from the benchmark could lead to the development of novel training techniques and architectures specifically tailored to handle continuous nuisance shifts. The continuous measurement of robustness can also impact the selection of model deployment. This enables the development of more safe AI solutions.

*   **Justification of Score:** I assign a score of **8**. The paper provides a significant and novel contribution to the field of robustness evaluation. The continuous shift benchmark using LoRA's significantly enhances realism compared to existing methods. The insights from the large-scale study are valuable and the improved filtering mechanism is a practical improvement. The main weakness lies in the reliance on generative models, which are prone to biases and have limited fidelity, but the advantages outweighs the disadvantages. Its potential to influence future research and development in the field is considerable.
