This is achieved through a trustless system where the protected model remains entirely unmodified, guaranteeing absolute fidelity.  The paper proposes ShadowMark, a concrete implementation of NWaaS, which leverages a key encoder and watermark decoder to extract owner-defined watermarks from model outputs via a side channel in the model's black-box API.  The paper demonstrates the efficacy of ShadowMark through extensive experiments across various X-to-Image models and defends against surrogate attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the concept of non-intrusive watermarking, which departs significantly from existing approaches. The idea of extracting watermarks from unmodified model outputs by learning a mapping conditioned on secret keys is innovative.  The development of ShadowMark as a practical implementation is also valuable. It successfully bypasses limitations regarding trust with 3rd party entities, and successfully defends against surrogate attacks. 
* **Significance:** Existing watermarking schemes have inherent drawbacks due to their intrusive nature that induces risks for fidelity or reliability. NWaaS addresses this fundamental limitation, making watermarking potentially more acceptable to model owners. The trustless nature of the system and the potential for broad applicability across different X-to-Image models also contribute to its significance. The ShadowMark implementation proves its reliability and practicality and has a potential impact on the field.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Conceptual Breakthrough:** The non-intrusive approach is a significant departure from the norm.
    * **Practical Implementation:** ShadowMark provides a concrete and working example of the NWaaS concept.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  Experiments cover a wide range of X-to-Image models and demonstrate robustness against attacks.
    * **Trustless Design:** The trustless system resolves the issues surrounding the need to trust 3rd parties, which opens up a new possibility for watermarking solutions.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependence on Training Data:**  The performance of ShadowMark heavily relies on the quality and distribution of the training data used to train the encoder and decoder. The sensitivity analysis to the distribution of data would be beneficial for a more complete picture of the practical challenges.
    * **Secret Key security:** The authors address the possibility of brute force attack by increasing the length of keys, but more research is necessary to find effective ways to countermeasure secret keys attacks on watermarking models.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by shifting the focus from intrusive to non-intrusive watermarking methods.  It opens up avenues for future research in developing more sophisticated non-intrusive techniques and exploring their applicability to other types of DNN models. ShadowMark could become a valuable tool for protecting the intellectual property of X-to-Image models, especially in cloud-based service environments.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a compelling concept and backs it up with a well-designed implementation and thorough experimental validation. It directly tackles a major limitation of existing watermarking methods and offers a promising alternative. While the approach may have some limitations regarding robustness, and requiring future work in addressing key security, the novelty and potential impact justify a high score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### GrAInS: Gradient-based Attribution for Inference-Time Steering of LLMs and VLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18043v1)
- **Authors**: Duy Nguyen, Archiki Prasad, Elias Stengel-Eskin, Mohit Bansal
- **Abstract**: Inference-time steering methods offer a lightweight alternative to fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs) by modifying internal activations at test time without updating model weights. However, most existing approaches rely on fixed, global intervention vectors, overlook the causal influence of individual input tokens, and fail to leverage informative gradients from the model's logits, particularly in multimodal settings where visual and textual inputs contribute unevenly. To address these limitations, we introduce GrAInS, an inference-time steering approach that operates across both language-only and vision-language models and tasks. GrAInS uses contrastive, gradient-based attribution via Integrated Gradients to identify the top-k most influential tokens, both positively and negatively attributed based on their contribution to preferred versus dispreferred outputs. These tokens are then used to construct directional steering vectors that capture semantic shifts from undesirable to desirable behavior. During inference, GrAInS adjusts hidden activations at transformer layers guided by token-level attribution signals, and normalizes activations to preserve representational scale. This enables fine-grained, interpretable, and modular control over model behavior, without retraining or auxiliary supervision. Empirically, GrAInS consistently outperforms both fine-tuning and existing steering baselines: it achieves a 13.22% accuracy gain on TruthfulQA using Llama-3.1-8B, reduces hallucination rates on MMHal-Bench from 0.624 to 0.514 with LLaVA-1.6-7B, and improves alignment win rates on SPA-VL by 8.11%, all while preserving the model's fluency and general capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GRAINS: Gradient-based Attribution for Inference-Time Steering of LLMs and VLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GRAINS, a novel inference-time steering method for large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs).  Unlike existing methods that often rely on global intervention vectors and disregard the causal influence of individual input tokens, GRAINS leverages contrastive gradient-based attribution via Integrated Gradients to identify the most influential tokens (both positive and negative contributors to desired/undesired outputs). It then constructs directional steering vectors based on these tokens and adjusts hidden activations at transformer layers during inference, normalizing activations to maintain representational scale.  The method aims to provide fine-grained, interpretable, and modular control over model behavior without fine-tuning or auxiliary supervision.  Experiments across various tasks (TruthfulQA, MMHal-Bench, SPA-VL, Toxigen, FaithEval) demonstrate that GRAINS outperforms both fine-tuning and existing steering baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its integration of gradient-based attribution with activation steering.  While Integrated Gradients and inference-time steering are established techniques, the specific combination and application in the context of *selective*, token-aware intervention is a valuable contribution. Existing methods often use coarse-grained approaches, while GRAINS offers a more precise and interpretable steering mechanism. The use of both positive and negative attribution to create a more targeted steering direction is also a strong point. However, the individual components are not entirely new; the novelty is in the specific combination. The approach seems straightforward, raising questions on the level of contribution given the wide availability of these steering techniques.

*   **Significance:** The significance of GRAINS is in its potential to improve the safety, factuality, and alignment of LLMs and VLMs without the computational overhead and data requirements of fine-tuning. Its modular and interpretable nature can facilitate debugging and controlling model behavior, which is crucial for deploying these models in real-world applications. The improvements demonstrated on benchmarks like TruthfulQA and MMHal-Bench are substantial, suggesting a practical impact. The preservation of general capabilities (reasoning and fluency) alongside targeted improvements makes it even more significant. That being said, the impact might be limited if GRAINS is not easily adaptable to other tasks and models. While the claim to be model-agnostic is strong, the paper only shows some models from OpenAI are used.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong experimental results across diverse tasks and models.
    *   Clear and well-motivated approach.
    *   Improved interpretability and control compared to existing steering methods.
    *   Demonstrated preservation of general capabilities.
    *   Ablation studies that support the design choices of the method.
    *   Includes both language and vision tasks, thereby showing that there is validity across multiple fields

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on Integrated Gradients can be computationally expensive, especially for large models. The runtime details provided are for a single step and don't properly scale given that IG relies on several steps.
    *   The method still requires a dataset for constructing steering vectors, although smaller than what is required for fine-tuning.
    *   The ablation comparing to likelihood objective is not comprehensive. Adding a negative loss to the likelihood should add some stability to this loss and would lead to more confident scores.

*   **Potential Influence:** If GRAINS proves to be robust and easily adaptable to new tasks and models, it could become a standard technique for inference-time steering, influencing future research on safe and reliable LLM/VLM deployment. The approach of using attribution for fine-grained control could inspire new methods for model intervention and explainability.

*   **Overall:** While the components of GRAINS are not individually revolutionary, the specific combination and application of gradient-based attribution for token-aware activation steering offers a valuable and practical contribution to the field. The improvements shown on safety-critical tasks are compelling, and the interpretable nature of the method is a significant advantage. The paper's significance is somewhat limited by the continued reliance on a dataset for steering vector construction and the computational cost of Integrated Gradients.

**Score: 7**

*Justification:* The paper makes a solid contribution by combining existing techniques in a novel and effective way, leading to demonstrable improvements in safety and alignment. The gains demonstrated across multiple modalities are a strong selling point. However, the incremental nature of the novelty and the computational overhead of Integrated Gradients prevent a higher score. The theoretical contribution is minimal, but the practical impact is promising, especially if the method can be further optimized and generalized.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Synthetic Data Generation for Phrase Break Prediction with Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18044v1)
- **Authors**: Hoyeon Lee, Sejung Son, Ye-Eun Kang, Jong-Hwan Kim
- **Abstract**: Current approaches to phrase break prediction address crucial prosodic aspects of text-to-speech systems but heavily rely on vast human annotations from audio or text, incurring significant manual effort and cost. Inherent variability in the speech domain, driven by phonetic factors, further complicates acquiring consistent, high-quality data. Recently, large language models (LLMs) have shown success in addressing data challenges in NLP by generating tailored synthetic data while reducing manual annotation needs. Motivated by this, we explore leveraging LLM to generate synthetic phrase break annotations, addressing the challenges of both manual annotation and speech-related tasks by comparing with traditional annotations and assessing effectiveness across multiple languages. Our findings suggest that LLM-based synthetic data generation effectively mitigates data challenges in phrase break prediction and highlights the potential of LLMs as a viable solution for the speech domain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for generating synthetic data for phrase break prediction in text-to-speech (TTS) systems. It addresses the challenges of traditional manual annotation methods, which are costly, time-consuming, and prone to inconsistencies, particularly across multiple languages. The authors propose leveraging LLMs to generate phrase break annotations, comparing them with audio-oriented and text-oriented human annotations. The results demonstrate that LLM-generated data can achieve quality comparable to human annotations, offering a more consistent and cost-efficient approach. Furthermore, the paper explores cross-lingual knowledge transfer to extend the method to languages with fewer resources, like French and Spanish, by incorporating English examples into the prompt. Finally, the synthetic data is used to train a smaller model to assess its practical value in replacing human annotations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in applying LLM-based synthetic data generation to the relatively unexplored domain of phrase break prediction within speech processing. While LLMs have been used for synthetic data in text-based tasks, this is the first study explicitly investigating their utility for this specific speech-related task, addressing the unique challenges of capturing prosodic information. The cross-lingual adaptation using a minimal set of examples is also a novel approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper holds significance for several reasons:

    *   **Addressing Data Scarcity:** It provides a practical solution to the problem of data scarcity in phrase break prediction, especially for low-resource languages. This can enable the development of better TTS systems for a wider range of languages.

    *   **Cost-Efficiency:** The proposed method significantly reduces the manual effort and costs associated with human annotation, making it more accessible to researchers and developers with limited resources.

    *   **Consistency:** LLM-generated data is shown to be more consistent than human-annotated data, which can lead to improved model performance.

    *   **Cross-lingual Transfer:** The study demonstrates the feasibility of cross-lingual knowledge transfer, further reducing the need for language-specific annotations.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the problem of data scarcity and annotation challenges in phrase break prediction.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:** The study employs a comprehensive evaluation methodology, comparing LLM-generated data with different types of human annotations across multiple languages.
    *   **Practical Validation:**  Training a smaller model using the synthetic data and evaluating its performance provides practical validation of the approach's effectiveness.
    *   **Clear Writing and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the methodology, results, and conclusions.
    *   **Addressing an Open Problem:** Synthetic data in TTS front-end processing is a relatively unexplored area, marking this work as a forward-looking and important contribution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on GPT-4-mini:** While cost-effective, using GPT-4-mini for the bulk of the annotation is a weakness. The results may be more significant had the authors used a more open-source model or a better benchmark.
    *   **Limited Domain of Evaluation:**  The paper primarily evaluates the method on data from the same domain (news, community websites). Evaluating on a wider range of domains (e.g., conversational speech, children's stories) would further demonstrate the generalizability of the approach.
    *   **Simplified Phrase Break Labels:** The use of a limited set of phrase break labels (AP, IP, SB) may not capture the full nuances of prosody. Exploring more fine-grained labels or hierarchical structures could be a valuable extension.
    *   **Lack of Error Analysis (Details):** While some error analysis is conducted, a more detailed examination of the types of errors made by the LLM would provide insights into its limitations and potential areas for improvement. For example, the paper mentions that Zero-Shot misplaced break symbols. More detail is needed.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in several ways:

    *   **Encouraging the use of LLMs for synthetic data generation in speech processing.**
    *   **Developing more sophisticated prompting strategies for capturing prosodic information.**
    *   **Exploring cross-lingual transfer learning for low-resource TTS.**
    *   **Investigating the impact of synthetic data on other aspects of TTS, such as intonation and speaker style.**

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper makes a valuable and novel contribution by applying LLMs to synthetic data generation for phrase break prediction. The results are promising, demonstrating the potential of this approach to address data scarcity and reduce annotation costs. The cross-lingual adaptation is also a significant contribution. While the paper has some limitations in terms of the specific LLM used and the domain of evaluation, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses. It opens up new avenues for research in TTS and provides a practical solution for improving TTS systems for low-resource languages.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### RECALLED: An Unbounded Resource Consumption Attack on Large Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18053v1)
- **Authors**: Haoran Gao, Yuanhe Zhang, Zhenhong Zhou, Lei Jiang, Fanyu Meng, Yujia Xiao, Kun Wang, Yang Liu, Junlan Feng
- **Abstract**: Resource Consumption Attacks (RCAs) have emerged as a significant threat to the deployment of Large Language Models (LLMs). With the integration of vision modalities, additional attack vectors exacerbate the risk of RCAs in large vision-language models (LVLMs). However, existing red-teaming studies have largely overlooked visual inputs as a potential attack surface, resulting in insufficient mitigation strategies against RCAs in LVLMs. To address this gap, we propose RECALLED (\textbf{RE}source \textbf{C}onsumption \textbf{A}ttack on \textbf{L}arge Vision-\textbf{L}anguag\textbf{E} Mo\textbf{D}els), the first approach for exploiting visual modalities to trigger unbounded RCAs red-teaming. First, we present \textit{Vision Guided Optimization}, a fine-grained pixel-level optimization, to obtain \textit{Output Recall} adversarial perturbations, which can induce repeating output. Then, we inject the perturbations into visual inputs, triggering unbounded generations to achieve the goal of RCAs. Additionally, we introduce \textit{Multi-Objective Parallel Losses} to generate universal attack templates and resolve optimization conflicts when intending to implement parallel attacks. Empirical results demonstrate that RECALLED increases service response latency by over 26 $\uparrow$, resulting in an additional 20\% increase in GPU utilization and memory consumption. Our study exposes security vulnerabilities in LVLMs and establishes a red-teaming framework that can facilitate future defense development against RCAs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RECALLED, a novel method for generating Resource Consumption Attacks (RCAs) against Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs). The attack leverages the visual modality by crafting adversarial perturbations that induce repetitive output generation, thereby exhausting computational resources. The method utilizes a Vision Guided Optimization technique with an Output Recall objective to create perturbations triggering non-terminating states. The paper also proposes a Multi-Objective Parallel Loss to generate universal attack templates. Experimental results on various LVLMs demonstrate the effectiveness of RECALLED in increasing inference latency, GPU utilization, and memory consumption. The paper highlights the vulnerability of LVLMs to visual input manipulation and establishes a red-teaming framework for future defense development.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic exploration of visual inputs as a viable attack surface for RCAs in LVLMs. While text-based RCAs are well-studied, the exploitation of the visual modality is a less explored area. The introduction of Vision Guided Optimization for crafting specific adversarial perturbations tailored for output recall is also novel. The Multi-Objective Parallel Loss function represents a useful, practical contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is two-fold:
    *   **Security Awareness:** It highlights a critical vulnerability in LVLMs that could lead to service degradation and system crashes. This is important as LVLMs become increasingly integrated into real-world applications.
    *   **Red-Teaming Framework:** The paper provides a practical framework for red-teaming LVLMs, enabling researchers and developers to identify and address potential security flaws.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a comprehensive evaluation of RECALLED across multiple LVLMs, demonstrating its effectiveness in various scenarios.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper includes a detailed analysis of the attack mechanisms and identifies the underlying vulnerabilities that contribute to the success of the RCA. The analysis of prediction tendencies, and the impact of temperature and repetition penalty hyperparameters on adversarial attacks.
    *   **Defense Exploration:** The paper explores potential defense measures, acknowledging their limitations and providing directions for future research.
    *   **Practical Implementation:** The algorithms and techniques are well-described, enabling researchers to replicate and extend the work.
    *   **Covertness analysis:** The experimental results are well designed, the covertness analysis demonstrate that even if people look at the image, they still cannot see it is harmful.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Defenses:** While the paper explores a defensive mechanism, it mainly focuses on performance analysis. The proposed defense is relatively simple and might be bypassable with more sophisticated attacks. A more in-depth exploration of robust defense strategies would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Real-World Deployment Simulations:**  The paper simulates resource consumption; however, it does not showcase the impact in production scale scenarios. Also the proposed attack would exhaust the computational resources, and might be easily observed with common metric dashboards.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:** While the authors claim to have followed responsible disclosure practices, the release of such an attack method could potentially be misused. A stronger discussion of ethical considerations and potential mitigation strategies is necessary.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by identifying and demonstrating a novel attack vector against LVLMs. The technical approach is sound, the experiments are comprehensive, and the analysis is insightful. However, the limited scope of defense exploration and the potential ethical concerns prevent it from receiving a higher score. Although this paper showcases the covertness attack that even if people look at the image, the image looks harmless, still there are limited evaluation in human-in-the-loop scenarios.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Privacy-Preserving Synthetic Review Generation with Diverse Writing Styles Using LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18055v1)
- **Authors**: Tevin Atwal, Chan Nam Tieu, Yefeng Yuan, Zhan Shi, Yuhong Liu, Liang Cheng
- **Abstract**: The increasing use of synthetic data generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) presents both opportunities and challenges in data-driven applications. While synthetic data provides a cost-effective, scalable alternative to real-world data to facilitate model training, its diversity and privacy risks remain underexplored. Focusing on text-based synthetic data, we propose a comprehensive set of metrics to quantitatively assess the diversity (i.e., linguistic expression, sentiment, and user perspective), and privacy (i.e., re-identification risk and stylistic outliers) of synthetic datasets generated by several state-of-the-art LLMs. Experiment results reveal significant limitations in LLMs' capabilities in generating diverse and privacy-preserving synthetic data. Guided by the evaluation results, a prompt-based approach is proposed to enhance the diversity of synthetic reviews while preserving reviewer privacy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the increasing use of synthetic data generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) for tasks like model training, focusing specifically on online product review data. It identifies that while synthetic data offers a cost-effective alternative to real-world data, it often lacks diversity and may expose privacy risks. The authors propose a comprehensive set of metrics to quantitatively evaluate the diversity (linguistic expression, sentiment, user perspective) and privacy (re-identification risk, stylistic outliers) of LLM-generated synthetic reviews. The metrics are applied to datasets generated by several state-of-the-art LLMs (GPT-4 and Claude 3.7 Sonnet). The authors find limitations in the diversity and privacy preservation capabilities of LLMs. To mitigate these limitations, they introduce a prompt-based approach with an automatic prompt optimization pipeline, adaptively refining generation instructions based on metric evaluation failures, to enhance diversity while preserving reviewer privacy. The approach dynamically updates prompts, resulting in higher-quality and more balanced synthetic review datasets compared to static prompting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Metrics:** The paper's main strength lies in its development and application of a diverse set of metrics covering both diversity and privacy aspects of synthetic review generation. The use of lexical richness, semantic diversity, sentiment diversity, and privacy risks (including both direct identifier and stylistic outlier detection) is well thought out and provides a multi-faceted evaluation approach.
    *   **Practical Problem:** The paper tackles a relevant and timely problem in the era of increasingly powerful LLMs: the need for diverse, high-quality, and privacy-preserving synthetic data. This issue has significant implications for various AI applications.
    *   **Prompt Optimization:** The proposed iterative prompt optimization pipeline is a promising approach. It allows for adaptive improvement in synthetic data generation without manual tuning, which makes it scalable and potentially applicable to other types of text data.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The empirical evaluation, using popular LLMs (GPT-4 and Claude), and comparison with a real-world dataset, provide valuable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of LLMs in this context.
    *   **Reproducible Methodology:** The paper clearly outlines the methodology, making it potentially reproducible by other researchers in the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty in Metrics:** While the combination of metrics is comprehensive, some of the individual metrics themselves (e.g., N-gram analysis, sentiment analysis) are well-established in NLP. The novelty primarily resides in the application and integration of these metrics within the specific context of synthetic review evaluation.
    *   **Prompt Engineering Heuristics:** The prompt optimization approach, although adaptive, relies on predefined prompt sets for each metric. This might limit the flexibility and effectiveness of the optimization process. More advanced reinforcement learning techniques or generative models could potentially yield better results.  The paper mentions RL as future work.
    *   **Dataset Limitations:** The paper focuses solely on Amazon product review data. While this is a rich and relevant dataset, the conclusions might not generalize well to other types of text data or different domains.
    *   **Stylistic Outlier Detection Assumptions:** The stylistic outlier detection relies on certain assumptions about writing style similarity and outlier definition. It would be beneficial to further validate these assumptions with user studies to ensure that identified outliers truly represent privacy risks.
    *   **Quantifiable Improvement:** While the paper demonstrates the effectiveness of the adaptive prompting, the paper does not perform any downstream task experiment to show how downstream model performance improves with this generated synthetic data.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper contributes to the growing body of research on synthetic data generation and evaluation.
    *   The evaluation metrics and methodology can serve as a benchmark for future research in this area.
    *   The prompt optimization approach provides a practical solution for enhancing the diversity and privacy of synthetic reviews.

**Overall:**

This paper provides a valuable contribution to the field by offering a comprehensive evaluation framework for assessing the diversity and privacy of LLM-generated synthetic review data. The prompt optimization approach is promising, although the novelty of individual metrics and reliance on manual prompting could be improved. The paper has the potential to influence future research and development of more robust and responsible synthetic data generation techniques. The paper is well-written and addresses a timely and important problem.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The score reflects the paper's strengths in providing a comprehensive evaluation methodology and a practical prompt optimization approach. While the novelty of individual components might be limited, the integration and application within the context of synthetic review generation are significant contributions. The weaknesses, particularly regarding the limited dataset and potential for more sophisticated prompt engineering methods, warrant a non-exceptional score. Nevertheless, the paper represents a valuable step forward in addressing the challenges of generating diverse, high-quality, and privacy-preserving synthetic data.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### BokehDiff: Neural Lens Blur with One-Step Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18060v1)
- **Authors**: Chengxuan Zhu, Qingnan Fan, Qi Zhang, Jinwei Chen, Huaqi Zhang, Chao Xu, Boxin Shi
- **Abstract**: We introduce BokehDiff, a novel lens blur rendering method that achieves physically accurate and visually appealing outcomes, with the help of generative diffusion prior. Previous methods are bounded by the accuracy of depth estimation, generating artifacts in depth discontinuities. Our method employs a physics-inspired self-attention module that aligns with the image formation process, incorporating depth-dependent circle of confusion constraint and self-occlusion effects. We adapt the diffusion model to the one-step inference scheme without introducing additional noise, and achieve results of high quality and fidelity. To address the lack of scalable paired data, we propose to synthesize photorealistic foregrounds with transparency with diffusion models, balancing authenticity and scene diversity.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** BokehDiff is a new method for lens blur rendering that leverages generative diffusion models to produce images with physically accurate and visually appealing lens blur effects. Unlike previous approaches that struggle due to limitations in depth estimation accuracy—often leading to artifacts at depth discontinuities—BokehDiff employs a physics-inspired self-attention mechanism that adheres to the image formation process. This approach integrates depth-dependent constraints and accounts for self-occlusion effects, improving the realism of the generated images. The authors also modify the diffusion model for one-step inference, eliminating the introduction of extra noise while maintaining high-quality output. To tackle the issue of limited scalable paired data, they propose the synthetic creation of photorealistic transparent foregrounds through diffusion models, enhancing both authenticity and diversity in scenes. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of computer graphics and computer vision, specifically in the context of image rendering and depth perception. The innovation of employing a physics-inspired self-attention mechanism is a strong point, as it enhances the fidelity of lens blur rendering by addressing the shortcomings of earlier methods that did not adequately consider depth-related phenomena. Additionally, the one-step diffusion model adaptation is a notable advancement over traditional approaches, which often involve more complex iterative sampling processes. However, several limitations deserve attention. The reliance on generative diffusion models and the need for synthetic data could undermine the practical applicability of BokehDiff. The performance and generalization of the method across a wide range of scenes and conditions are not thoroughly validated, which raises questions about its robustness in real-world applications. Furthermore, the paper could benefit from comparison with a wider range of existing methods to conclusively demonstrate its advantages. The potential influence of this research is substantial, as realistic lens blurring is an essential aspect of image processing that enhances viewer experience and fidelity in visual media. If future work demonstrates effective applications and solid validation in diverse scenarios, BokehDiff could reshape how depth-dependent effects are rendered in both graphics and vision. **Score: 7**   This score reflects solid novelty and a meaningful contribution to the field, tempered by unanswered questions surrounding generalizability and practical implementation.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### TELEVAL: A Dynamic Benchmark Designed for Spoken Language Models in Chinese Interactive Scenarios
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18061v1)
- **Authors**: Zehan Li, Hongjie Chen, Yuxin Zhang, Jing Zhou, Xuening Wang, Hang Lv, Mengjie Du, Yaodong Song, Jie Lian, Jian Kang, Jie Li, Yongxiang Li, Zhongjiang He, Xuelong Li
- **Abstract**: Spoken language models (SLMs) have seen rapid progress in recent years, along with the development of numerous benchmarks for evaluating their performance. However, most existing benchmarks primarily focus on evaluating whether SLMs can perform complex tasks comparable to those tackled by large language models (LLMs), often failing to align with how users naturally interact in real-world conversational scenarios. In this paper, we propose TELEVAL, a dynamic benchmark specifically designed to evaluate SLMs' effectiveness as conversational agents in realistic Chinese interactive settings. TELEVAL defines three evaluation dimensions: Explicit Semantics, Paralinguistic and Implicit Semantics, and System Abilities. It adopts a dialogue format consistent with real-world usage and evaluates text and audio outputs separately. TELEVAL particularly focuses on the model's ability to extract implicit cues from user speech and respond appropriately without additional instructions. Our experiments demonstrate that despite recent progress, existing SLMs still have considerable room for improvement in natural conversational tasks. We hope that TELEVAL can serve as a user-centered evaluation framework that directly reflects the user experience and contributes to the development of more capable dialogue-oriented SLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TELEVAL: A Dynamic Benchmark Designed for Spoken Language Models in Chinese Interactive Scenarios":

**Summary**

The paper introduces TELEVAL, a new dynamic benchmark designed to evaluate Spoken Language Models (SLMs) in realistic Chinese interactive scenarios. The benchmark addresses perceived limitations of existing benchmarks, particularly their mismatch with real-world conversational interactions.  TELEVAL focuses on three key dimensions: Explicit Semantics, Paralinguistic and Implicit Semantics (acoustic-level cues like emotion, age, intentions), and System Capabilities (robustness, latency, interruption handling). The benchmark uses a dialogue format, evaluates textual and audio outputs separately, and emphasizes the model's ability to extract implicit cues from user speech.  The paper details the construction of the dataset, including the acquisition of user audio (both synthesized and real recordings), and provides preliminary experimental results demonstrating that current SLMs, even advanced ones, still have room for improvement in natural conversational tasks. They offer TELEVAL as a user-centered framework reflecting real user experiences.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a valuable contribution by addressing the limitations of current benchmarks in evaluating SLMs for real-world conversational settings, particularly in Chinese. While many benchmarks exist, TELEVAL's emphasis on implicit cues, paralinguistic information, dialectal understanding, and system-level capabilities makes it distinct. This moves beyond basic comprehension tasks like question answering towards assessing more nuanced aspects of human-computer interaction. The separation of text and audio evaluation is also a plus for addressing potential ASR challenges.
    However, the approach is not entirely ground-breaking. Other benchmarks have considered some of these aspects; TELEVAL's novelty primarily lies in the combination and comprehensive coverage of these factors specifically within the Chinese language context.

*   **Significance:** TELEVAL is a potentially significant benchmark because it directly tackles a gap in SLM evaluation. The ability of SLMs to engage in natural, human-like conversations is essential for their practical application, but existing metrics often fail to capture this. A benchmark that focuses on real-world interaction, particularly taking implicit cues into account, can drive further research and development towards more effective conversational agents. The focus on Chinese scenarios is also significant, as it addresses a specific language context with unique linguistic features and cultural nuances.
* **Scope of Tasks**: The tasks chosen in the benchmark are very comprehensive. By evaluating speech from the perspective of dialect comprehension, human values, and human-likeness, the benchmark touches upon the complex characteristics of human language and how machines can imitate it in a robust manner.

*   **Limitations & Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper presents a first release of the benchmark, but leaves room for expansion and updates. This means the current version may still be limited in certain areas.
    *   Although synthesized audio is used alongside real recordings, there may exist gaps between the human-like quality of synthesized data and natural human speech. The potential influence of this difference is important to consider.
    *   The reliance on LLM-as-judges for some evaluations raises concerns about potential biases and inconsistencies, even with carefully designed prompts. The time-sensitivity of certain knowledge-based questions is also a limitation.
    *   The results section is somewhat preliminary. While the results suggest room for improvement, it lacks a deep dive analysis of the specific strengths and weaknesses of different models on particular TELEVAL tasks. More thorough error analysis and insights would strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The benchmark has the potential to become widely used in the SLM research community, especially for researchers working on Chinese dialogue systems. It offers a valuable tool for evaluating and comparing different models, as well as for identifying areas where further improvement is needed. The detailed data construction methodology and evaluation metrics should facilitate reproducible research.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant benchmark that addresses a key limitation in SLM evaluation. TELEVAL's comprehensive approach to evaluating conversational abilities, particularly in Chinese, is valuable and timely. However, the benchmark is still in its early stages, and the results section could be more in-depth. The potential limitations related to synthesized audio and LLM-based evaluations also warrant caution. Nevertheless, TELEVAL has the potential to significantly influence the field by driving progress towards more human-like conversational SLMs.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Group Sequence Policy Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18071v1)
- **Authors**: Chujie Zheng, Shixuan Liu, Mingze Li, Xiong-Hui Chen, Bowen Yu, Chang Gao, Kai Dang, Yuqiong Liu, Rui Men, An Yang, Jingren Zhou, Junyang Lin
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), our stable, efficient, and performant reinforcement learning algorithm for training large language models. Unlike previous algorithms that adopt token-level importance ratios, GSPO defines the importance ratio based on sequence likelihood and performs sequence-level clipping, rewarding, and optimization. We demonstrate that GSPO achieves superior training efficiency and performance compared to the GRPO algorithm, notably stabilizes Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) RL training, and has the potential for simplifying the design of RL infrastructure. These merits of GSPO have contributed to the remarkable improvements in the latest Qwen3 models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), a new reinforcement learning (RL) algorithm designed for training large language models (LLMs). GSPO addresses stability issues observed in previous methods like GRPO, which the authors attribute to the misapplication of importance sampling at the token level.  Instead, GSPO defines the importance ratio based on sequence likelihood and performs sequence-level clipping, rewarding, and optimization. The authors demonstrate that GSPO offers superior training efficiency, stability, and performance compared to GRPO, particularly in the context of training Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models, and has been deployed successfully in Qwen3 models. GSPO also enables simpler RL infrastructure setup by obviating the need to recompute likelihoods with the training engine.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in redefining the importance ratio in RL for LLMs at the *sequence* level rather than the token level. This is presented as a theoretically sound correction to address the inherent instability of token-level importance sampling, especially when training large models with long sequences. The GSPO-token variant which introduces token level advantage customization to the framework adds another layer of novelty to the overall method. While PPO and GRPO existed prior, the specific application and modification for *sequence-level* optimization in the context of LLMs represents a notable contribution. The approach of using a group of responses to compute advantages relative to each other rather than a learned value function has also been present in other works; this paper integrates sequence-level likelihood optimization with that benefit.

* **Significance:**  The significance is high if the claims hold true and replicate across diverse LLM architectures. The observation that token-level importance sampling introduces noise and instability, particularly in MoE models, is a critical insight. Addressing this could significantly improve the training of LLMs via RL, leading to better performance and capabilities. The ability to stabilize MoE training *without* complex workarounds like "Routing Replay" represents a major practical advantage. The simplification of the RL infrastructure is also important for scalability. Furthermore, if the GSPO algorithm is integrated directly with the generation engines, it would enable several use-cases such as more efficient partial rollout and multi-turn RL. The experimental results show substantial performance improvements on a diverse set of benchmarks, reinforcing the applicability of the presented method.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing approaches (GRPO) and provides a compelling explanation for the source of instability.
    * **Theoretically Grounded Solution:**  The sequence-level optimization is presented as a more theoretically sound application of importance sampling.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement in training stability, efficiency, and benchmark performance.
    * **Addressing a Practical Challenge:** The paper tackles a real-world issue faced when scaling RL for LLMs, particularly MoE models, a critical aspect of large-scale language model development.
    * **Infrastructure Simplification:** The proposed method significantly simplifies the RL infrastructure for LLMs.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Generalizability:** While the results are promising, there is a need for rigorous experimental validation to further justify the general applicability of this method across diverse LLM architectures and tasks.
    * **Limited ablation studies:** More rigorous ablation studies of the various components of the GSPO algorithm would strengthen the claims. It would be helpful to see how the sequence-level optimization performs compared to token level in the *absence* of MoEs and the stability issue and determine if the advantage is only visible when there is a training dynamics issue.

* **Potential Influence:** If GSPO proves to be a robust and generalizable solution, it could become a standard approach for RL fine-tuning of LLMs. This would significantly impact the development and deployment of more capable and reliable language models. The proposed solution may also influence a shift in how RL algorithms are designed and applied to LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8**. The paper offers a novel and well-reasoned solution to a significant problem in the RL training of LLMs. The theoretical justification and empirical results support the claim that GSPO provides superior training stability and performance. The simplification of RL infrastructure and the effectiveness in MoE models are additional strengths.

However, a few limitations temper the score. Further validation across diverse architectures and tasks is needed to ensure the generalizability of GSPO. More in-depth ablation studies would further solidify the findings. Nonetheless, the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Squeeze10-LLM: Squeezing LLMs' Weights by 10 Times via a Staged Mixed-Precision Quantization Method
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18073v1)
- **Authors**: Qingcheng Zhu, Yangyang Ren, Linlin Yang, Mingbao Lin, Yanjing Li, Sheng Xu, Zichao Feng, Haodong Zhu, Yuguang Yang, Juan Zhang, Runqi Wang, Baochang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Deploying large language models (LLMs) is challenging due to their massive parameters and high computational costs. Ultra low-bit quantization can significantly reduce storage and accelerate inference, but extreme compression (i.e., mean bit-width <= 2) often leads to severe performance degradation. To address this, we propose Squeeze10-LLM, effectively "squeezing" 16-bit LLMs' weights by 10 times. Specifically, Squeeze10-LLM is a staged mixed-precision post-training quantization (PTQ) framework and achieves an average of 1.6 bits per weight by quantizing 80% of the weights to 1 bit and 20% to 4 bits. We introduce Squeeze10LLM with two key innovations: Post-Binarization Activation Robustness (PBAR) and Full Information Activation Supervision (FIAS). PBAR is a refined weight significance metric that accounts for the impact of quantization on activations, improving accuracy in low-bit settings. FIAS is a strategy that preserves full activation information during quantization to mitigate cumulative error propagation across layers. Experiments on LLaMA and LLaMA2 show that Squeeze10-LLM achieves state-of-the-art performance for sub-2bit weight-only quantization, improving average accuracy from 43% to 56% on six zero-shot classification tasks--a significant boost over existing PTQ methods. Our code will be released upon publication.
- **Summary**: The paper "Squeeze10-LLM: Squeezing LLMs' Weights by 10 Times via a Staged Mixed-Precision Quantization Method" proposes a novel quantization method aimed at reducing the storage and computational costs associated with deploying large language models (LLMs). The method, named Squeeze10-LLM, utilizes a staged mixed-precision post-training quantization (PTQ) framework. It quantizes 80% of the weights to 1 bit and the remaining 20% to 4 bits, achieving an average of 1.6 bits per weight. The two main innovations are Post-Binarization Activation Robustness (PBAR) and Full Information Activation Supervision (FIAS). PBAR refines the weight significance metric by considering the impact of quantization on activations. FIAS preserves full activation information during quantization to prevent error propagation across layers. The authors demonstrate that Squeeze10-LLM achieves state-of-the-art performance for sub-2bit weight-only quantization on LLaMA and LLaMA2 models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of PBAR and FIAS within a staged mixed-precision PTQ framework. While mixed-precision quantization and activation-aware quantization techniques exist, the specific combination and implementation detailed in the paper appear to be new. PBAR seems to be a novel activation-aware metric. FIAS, leveraging original activations for supervision, is also a distinct approach. The specific staging and allocation of bits also contribute to the method's novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential to enable the deployment of LLMs on resource-constrained devices. Achieving 10x compression with minimal performance degradation is a substantial contribution to the field. The empirical results on LLaMA and LLaMA2 models highlight the method's effectiveness. The improvement over existing sub-2bit quantization methods is noteworthy. The reported accuracy increase on zero-shot classification tasks, coupled with the low bit-width, suggests this is a potentially valuable contribution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is well-motivated by the practical challenges of deploying LLMs.
    *   The paper clearly describes the Squeeze10-LLM framework and its components (PBAR and FIAS).
    *   The empirical results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance compared to existing ultra-low-bit quantization methods.
    *   Ablation studies are performed to demonstrate the contribution of PBAR and FIAS.
    *   The paper discusses limitations and provides sufficient details for reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper presents good results, there could be some additional analysis. For instance, examining the distribution of retained 4-bit weights might be insightful. It would be worth knowing if, in a given layer, the weights selected are localized (e.g. structured sparsity) or distributed randomly.
    *   The hyperparameter tuning could be more extensive. While the paper does tune the lambda value, a deeper analysis would be helpful.
    *   Generalization to other LLM architectures and tasks beyond those presented needs to be further investigated in future work.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by providing an effective method for compressing LLMs without substantial performance loss. This could lead to more widespread adoption of LLMs on edge devices and in other resource-constrained environments. The PBAR and FIAS techniques could also be adopted and adapted by other researchers in the quantization and compression space.

**Rationale:**

While mixed-precision quantization and activation-aware quantization methods are not entirely new, the specific combination of techniques, especially PBAR and FIAS, along with the staged mixed-precision framework and demonstrably improved empirical results, warrants a high score. The potential impact on deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments is substantial. However, the relatively narrow scope of evaluation and opportunities for further analysis, prevent this work from reaching a score close to a 10.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Hybrid and Unitary Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models: Methods and Benchmarking under Resource Constraints
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18076v1)
- **Authors**: Haomin Qi, Zihan Dai, Chengbo Huang
- **Abstract**: Fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) remains a computational bottleneck due to their scale and memory demands. This paper presents a comprehensive evaluation of parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) techniques, including LoRA, BOFT, LoRA-GA, and uRNN, and introduces a novel hybrid strategy that dynamically integrates BOFT's orthogonal stability with LoRA-GA's gradient-aligned rapid convergence. By computing per-layer adaptive updates guided by gradient norms, the hybrid method achieves superior convergence efficiency and generalization across diverse tasks. We also explore, for the first time, the adaptation of unitary RNN (uRNN) principles to transformer-based LLMs, enhancing gradient stability through structured unitary constraints. Empirical evaluations on four benchmarks -- GLUE, GSM8K, MT-Bench, and HumanEval -- using models ranging from 7B to 405B parameters demonstrate that our hybrid method consistently outperforms individual PEFT baselines, approaching full fine-tuning accuracy while reducing resource consumption by up to 2.1 times in training time and 50 percent in memory usage. These findings establish the hybrid approach as a practical and scalable fine-tuning solution for real-world deployment of LLMs under resource constraints.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the computational challenges of fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) by proposing a hybrid parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) strategy. The approach dynamically integrates the strengths of LoRA-GA (fast convergence) and BOFT (orthogonal stability) using a gradient-norm-based adaptive weighting scheme at the per-layer level. Furthermore, the paper explores the incorporation of unitary RNN (uRNN) principles into transformer-based LLMs to enhance gradient stability. Through experiments on various benchmarks and model sizes, the proposed hybrid method demonstrates superior performance compared to individual PEFT baselines, approaching full fine-tuning accuracy while reducing training time and memory usage.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in two primary aspects:

1.  **The Hybrid PEFT Approach:** Dynamically integrating LoRA-GA and BOFT based on gradient norms is a conceptually novel idea.  While prior works have explored different PEFT strategies, the dynamic, gradient-aware combination is a worthwhile contribution.  It intelligently exploits the complementary advantages of different methods, leading to a more robust and efficient fine-tuning process.
2.  **uRNN Adaptation to Transformers:** The adaptation of uRNN principles, traditionally used in recurrent networks, to transformer architectures for LLM fine-tuning is a relatively unexplored area. Replacing weight matrices in attention or feed-forward layers with structured unitary matrices parameterized by Fourier transforms and Householder reflections to enhance gradient stability is a novel idea. The structured parametrization of unitary matrices is a clever trick as it reduces parameters compared to a full matrix and it eases the computation in each forward-backward pass.

**Significance:** The potential significance of the paper is high, given the practical challenges of LLM fine-tuning.  The demonstrated improvements in training time and memory usage, coupled with competitive or even superior performance on benchmarks, are important.  A successful hybrid PEFT strategy and a reasonable transformer adaptation of uRNNs can make LLMs more accessible for real-world deployment under resource constraints.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results across diverse benchmarks and model sizes, supporting the effectiveness of the proposed hybrid method. The performance gains over individual PEFT baselines are consistently demonstrated. This gives credibility to the proposed method.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:** The methodology is clearly explained, including the mathematical formulations of the hybrid strategy and the uRNN adaptation.
*   **Addresses a Practical Problem:** LLM fine-tuning is a critical bottleneck in deploying these models, and the paper directly tackles this issue.
*   **Good Ablation studies:** Provide insight into each component within the hybrid method by contrasting against LORA-GA, BOFT, and other baselines.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited analysis for uRNN experiments:** The paper spends significant effort presenting the uRNN adaptation, but the performance improvements observed (if any) don't seem to warrant the complexity. The paper acknowledges limitations when it comes to multilingual task, but more analysis would be needed to judge whether it is a promising direction.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** PEFT methods, in general, can be sensitive to hyperparameter tuning. The paper mentions tuning, but it would be beneficial to provide more details about the tuning process and the potential sensitivity of the results. Also, did the tuning account for each component's interplay, such as whether dynamically adjusting the gradient weighting factor at affects the optimization?
*   **Computational Overhead (uRNN):** While PEFT aims for efficiency, the implementation of unitary matrices, particularly with Fourier transforms, might introduce computational overhead. The paper should elaborate on this aspect and compare the computational costs of uRNN-enhanced fine-tuning with other methods.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**
The paper presents a worthwhile and well-executed contribution to the field of parameter-efficient LLM fine-tuning. The dynamic hybrid strategy is novel and effective, and the experiments are thorough and convincing. However, the limited evidence of strong results for the uRNN adaptation, along with concerns about hyperparameter sensitivity and potential computational overhead, prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. If the authors can better justify the value of adding uRNN by showcasing how it can be incorporated into the hybrid model and how it overcomes the performance shortcomings of the hybrid model by itself, this paper would score an 8 or 9.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Understanding the Supply Chain and Risks of Large Language Model Applications
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18105v1)
- **Authors**: Yujie Ma, Lili Quan, Xiaofei Xie, Qiang Hu, Jiongchi Yu, Yao Zhang, Sen Chen
- **Abstract**: The rise of Large Language Models (LLMs) has led to the widespread deployment of LLM-based systems across diverse domains. As these systems proliferate, understanding the risks associated with their complex supply chains is increasingly important. LLM-based systems are not standalone as they rely on interconnected supply chains involving pretrained models, third-party libraries, datasets, and infrastructure. Yet, most risk assessments narrowly focus on model or data level, overlooking broader supply chain vulnerabilities. While recent studies have begun to address LLM supply chain risks, there remains a lack of benchmarks for systematic research. To address this gap, we introduce the first comprehensive dataset for analyzing and benchmarking LLM supply chain security. We collect 3,859 real-world LLM applications and perform interdependency analysis, identifying 109,211 models, 2,474 datasets, and 9,862 libraries. We extract model fine-tuning paths, dataset reuse, and library reliance, mapping the ecosystem's structure. To evaluate security, we gather 1,555 risk-related issues-50 for applications, 325 for models, 18 for datasets, and 1,229 for libraries from public vulnerability databases. Using this dataset, we empirically analyze component dependencies and risks. Our findings reveal deeply nested dependencies in LLM applications and significant vulnerabilities across the supply chain, underscoring the need for comprehensive security analysis. We conclude with practical recommendations to guide researchers and developers toward safer, more trustworthy LLM-enabled systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLMSCBench, the first comprehensive dataset for analyzing and benchmarking the security of Large Language Model (LLM) supply chains. The dataset encompasses a large collection of real-world LLM applications, their constituent components (models, datasets, libraries), and their interdependencies. It also includes risk-related issues extracted from vulnerability databases and repositories for each component.  The authors use this dataset to conduct an empirical study analyzing the dependencies and associated risks within LLM-based applications, revealing vulnerabilities across the LLM supply chain.  Finally, they provide practical implications and recommendations for researchers and developers aimed at building safer and more trustworthy LLM-enabled systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The LLMSCBench dataset is a significant contribution.  The lack of readily available datasets for LLM supply chain security is a major impediment to research in this area.  LLMSCBench provides a valuable resource for systematically studying dependencies and risks, acting as a benchmark for other researchers. The effort to collect, clean, and organize the data is commendable.
    *   **Holistic View:**  The paper takes a holistic view of LLM security, moving beyond model-centric approaches to consider the entire supply chain.  This broader perspective is essential, as vulnerabilities in datasets, libraries, and application code can have significant impacts.
    *   **Empirical Analysis:**  The empirical study reveals valuable insights into the complexity and risks within LLM applications. Identifying that popular models and libraries are heavily reused and/or depended upon underscores the critical need for thorough security analysis. Identifying specific risk areas (security, hallucination, privacy) and demonstrating vulnerabilities in libraries are helpful.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The paper concludes with practical implications for various stakeholders, including developers, model and dataset providers, and researchers. The recommendations are grounded in the empirical findings and offer concrete guidance.
    *   **Timeliness and Relevance:** The topic is timely and highly relevant.  As LLMs become more widely adopted, securing their supply chains becomes increasingly crucial.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Limitations:** While significant, LLMSCBench isn't perfect.  The collection process primarily relies on information from Hugging Face and related resources. This could introduce bias, as the dataset might not fully represent the broader landscape of LLM applications. Furthermore, the extraction of dataset usage information heavily relies on descriptions, which are not always accurate or complete. This is acknowledged by the authors. The extraction of risk issues from user discussions, which is necessarily subjective, introduces noise into the dataset.
    *   **Depth of Risk Analysis:** While the paper highlights several risks, the analysis could be more in-depth. For example, showing how vulnerabilities propagate through the supply chain, demonstrating the concrete impact of these vulnerabilities on real-world applications, and benchmarking existing mitigation strategies would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited Dataset Discussion:** While describing the distribution characteristics of the risk issues across the dataset, the authors do not relate the findings to concrete examples. This potentially hinders the reader from understanding how the characteristics may translate to real-world exploits, which can be useful for practitioners.
    *   **Lack of a Comprehensive Evaluation Methodology**: The authors performed analysis using a manual annotation approach. In reality, the community might not agree on what constitutes a risk or how it can be mapped to the dataset. A more thorough approach towards validating the risks would significantly improve the paper.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the construction and release of the LLMSCBench dataset itself. While individual components (analyzing LLM security, examining dataset vulnerabilities) have been explored before, the combination of a comprehensive dataset and a holistic supply chain perspective represents a new contribution.
    *   **Significance:** The significance stems from the growing importance of LLM security and the lack of existing resources for systematic research. LLMSCBench addresses this gap and facilitates future research in this area. The insights from the initial empirical study provide a valuable starting point for further investigation.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution, primarily due to the creation and release of the LLMSCBench dataset. This tangible contribution provides a foundation for future research and moves the field forward. However, the analysis, while informative, is somewhat limited in depth, and the dataset collection/validation methods have potential biases. While the analysis identified useful vulnerabilities and observations, it would benefit from incorporating concrete examples in their discussion. Considering these factors, a score of 7 is appropriate.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning of 3D DDPM for MRI Image Generation Using Tensor Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18112v1)
- **Authors**: Binghua Li, Ziqing Chang, Tong Liang, Chao Li, Toshihisa Tanaka, Shigeki Aoki, Qibin Zhao, Zhe Sun
- **Abstract**: We address the challenge of parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) for three-dimensional (3D) U-Net-based denoising diffusion probabilistic models (DDPMs) in magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) image generation. Despite its practical significance, research on parameter-efficient representations of 3D convolution operations remains limited. To bridge this gap, we propose Tensor Volumetric Operator (TenVOO), a novel PEFT method specifically designed for fine-tuning DDPMs with 3D convolutional backbones. Leveraging tensor network modeling, TenVOO represents 3D convolution kernels with lower-dimensional tensors, effectively capturing complex spatial dependencies during fine-tuning with few parameters. We evaluate TenVOO on three downstream brain MRI datasets-ADNI, PPMI, and BraTS2021-by fine-tuning a DDPM pretrained on 59,830 T1-weighted brain MRI scans from the UK Biobank. Our results demonstrate that TenVOO achieves state-of-the-art performance in multi-scale structural similarity index measure (MS-SSIM), outperforming existing approaches in capturing spatial dependencies while requiring only 0.3% of the trainable parameters of the original model. Our code is available at: https://github.com/xiaovhua/tenvoo
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) for 3D U-Net based Denoising Diffusion Probabilistic Models (DDPMs) used in MRI image generation. It proposes a novel PEFT method called Tensor Volumetric Operator (TenVOO), which leverages tensor network modeling to represent 3D convolution kernels with lower-dimensional tensors. This approach aims to capture complex spatial dependencies efficiently during fine-tuning using fewer parameters. The method is evaluated on brain MRI datasets (ADNI, PPMI, and BraTS2021) by fine-tuning a DDPM pretrained on a large UK Biobank dataset. The results demonstrate that TenVOO achieves state-of-the-art performance in multi-scale structural similarity index measure (MS-SSIM) with significantly fewer trainable parameters compared to existing approaches. The code is publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the application of tensor networks for parameter-efficient fine-tuning of 3D convolutional layers within a U-Net architecture for MRI image generation. While tensor network methods have been used in other domains (e.g., for transformers and image generation more broadly), their application to 3D convolutions in this specific medical imaging context appears to be a unique contribution. The proposed TenVOO method provides a specific way to decompose and represent the 3D convolutional filters, potentially allowing for better capture of spatial dependencies compared to simpler low-rank approaches.
*   **Significance:** Parameter-efficient fine-tuning is crucial for adapting large deep learning models to specific datasets, especially in medical imaging where data can be limited. The ability to efficiently fine-tune 3D DDPMs for MRI generation has practical implications for tasks such as disease prediction, diagnosis, and treatment planning. TenVOO's ability to achieve strong performance with a small number of trainable parameters (around 0.3%) makes it a potentially valuable tool for researchers and clinicians working with limited resources. The gains in MS-SSIM metrics suggests a better preservation of structural details in generated images, a key requirement for medical applications.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined goals.
    *   Novel application of tensor networks to a specific problem.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on multiple datasets.
    *   Demonstrated state-of-the-art performance in MS-SSIM.
    *   Significant reduction in trainable parameters.
    *   Publicly available code.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could be further strengthened by more in-depth analysis of TenVOO's limitations. For example, are there specific types of MRI data or anatomical structures where it performs less well? Are there any particular parameter settings of TenVOO that lead to instability?
    *   The results on PPMI weren't as promising as for the other two datasets, suggesting there's a sensitivity to the type of data being generated. This aspect deserves further explanation.
    *   While MS-SSIM results are good, more thorough analysis and discussion of generated images compared to the original is merited. Qualitative results are limited to visual inspection which is subjective.

**Justification for the score:**

The paper presents a novel method (TenVOO) for PEFT of 3D convolutional networks, tailored to the specific domain of MRI image generation. The achieved improvements in MS-SSIM, the clear parameter reduction, and the experimental validation using multiple datasets supports the method's effectiveness. However, limitations should be stated more clearly, as well as shortcomings in some datasets. While the technical contribution is relatively contained (application of an existing technology), its practical implications in the area of medical imaging PEFT makes it valuable.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Policy Disruption in Reinforcement Learning:Adversarial Attack with Large Language Models and Critical State Identification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18113v1)
- **Authors**: Junyong Jiang, Buwei Tian, Chenxing Xu, Songze Li, Lu Dong
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning (RL) has achieved remarkable success in fields like robotics and autonomous driving, but adversarial attacks designed to mislead RL systems remain challenging. Existing approaches often rely on modifying the environment or policy, limiting their practicality. This paper proposes an adversarial attack method in which existing agents in the environment guide the target policy to output suboptimal actions without altering the environment. We propose a reward iteration optimization framework that leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate adversarial rewards explicitly tailored to the vulnerabilities of the target agent, thereby enhancing the effectiveness of inducing the target agent toward suboptimal decision-making. Additionally, a critical state identification algorithm is designed to pinpoint the target agent's most vulnerable states, where suboptimal behavior from the victim leads to significant degradation in overall performance. Experimental results in diverse environments demonstrate the superiority of our method over existing approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes ARCS, a novel adversarial attack framework against reinforcement learning (RL) agents in black-box settings. ARCS consists of two main components: (1) an LLM-guided reward iteration optimization module that adaptively generates customized adversarial reward functions tailored to the vulnerabilities of the target policy, and (2) a critical state identification mechanism that pinpoints states where suboptimal actions by the victim lead to significant performance degradation. The attacker then focuses on exploiting these critical states.  The framework aims to induce suboptimal actions in the target policy without directly manipulating the environment or having white-box access to the agent's internals. Experiments across various MuJoCo environments and autonomous driving scenarios demonstrate that ARCS outperforms existing adversarial policy training methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of LLMs for generating targeted adversarial rewards and the identification of critical states to focus the attack.  While adversarial attacks on RL are not new, the adaptive and automated nature of the reward design, coupled with the critical state exploitation, represents a step forward. The idea of using LLMs to guide adversarial reward design is interesting, as it shifts the burden of manual crafting. The state identification component seems also promising, it can potentially bring significant improvement to the attacking strategies. However, there is no explicit analysis of the LLM's generated rewards and how these are linked to the victim's policy's vulnerabilities. The paper does not show any analysis of the diversity of the LLM output. The work is not groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing the limitations of existing adversarial attack methods that often require unrealistic access to the environment or agent's policy.  ARCS operates in a more realistic black-box setting, enhancing its applicability. The experimental results show convincing improvements over baselines. Furthermore, such tools are very useful for stress-testing RL algorithms. The critical state identification is helpful for better safety. The limitation of the presented approach resides in its efficiency. The paper does not analyze the actual computational gain obtained by focusing on critical states.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The approach is well-motivated, addressing limitations of existing adversarial attack methods.
    *   The integration of LLMs for adaptive reward design is a novel contribution.
    *   The critical state identification mechanism provides a targeted approach to adversarial exploitation.
    *   The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of ARCS over baselines across diverse environments.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experimental setup is limited to simulated environments. The real-world transferability of the generated adversarial policies remains an open question.
    *   The paper lacks a detailed analysis of how the LLM-generated rewards specifically target the victim's vulnerabilities. More insights into the types of rewards and their impact on the victim's behavior would strengthen the analysis.
    *   The critical state is computed via a first-order approximation of the objective function.
    *   The performance improvement might not be sufficient given that multiple modules of the framework involve LLMs. A study of the actual computational cost of the framework is missing.

*   **Potential Influence:** ARCS could influence future research by encouraging the use of LLMs for generating adaptive adversarial strategies and exploring targeted attacks focused on critical states. The framework can be used for benchmarking RL robustness. The approach can encourage stress-testing of RL algorithms.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:**

While the paper presents a novel and well-motivated approach with promising experimental results, it has several limitations. The novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary, and the lack of deeper analysis of the LLM-generated rewards and the computational cost of the framework diminishes the potential impact. The reliance on simulated environments limits the assessment of its real-world applicability. For these reasons, the paper, while solid, does not warrant a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### NoCode-bench: A Benchmark for Evaluating Natural Language-Driven Feature Addition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18130v1)
- **Authors**: Le Deng, Zhonghao Jiang, Jialun Cao, Michael Pradel, Zhongxin Liu
- **Abstract**: Natural language-driven no-code development allows users to specify software functionality using natural language (NL) instead of editing source code, promising increased productivity and democratized development. Large language models (LLMs) show potential in enabling this paradigm. In this context, software documentation acts as an NL specification for functionality. This work introduces NoCode-bench, a benchmark designed to evaluate LLMs on real-world NL-driven feature addition tasks, consisting of 634 tasks across 10 projects and 114k code changes.
